\doxysubsection*{\+: Name of this operation state (usually xas).}
\label{_amgrp12cf68b3e9686647d8092153c6e95b25}%
\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_af36dcc4b1105372de5b2df4df450ca10}{X\+A\+\_\+\+S\+T\+A\+T\+E\+\_\+\+O\+R\+D\+E\+R()}} -\/ Declare an X\+Array operation state.

@array\+: Array to operate on. @index\+: Initial index of interest. @order\+: Order of entry.

Declare and initialise an \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} on the stack. {\footnotesize\ttfamily \#define xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each(\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{}]{xa,  }\item[{}]{\mbox{\hyperlink{kvm_2svm_2pmu_8c_a6053293001e6dbdc266facbd3024c21d}{index}},  }\item[{}]{entry }\end{DoxyParamCaption})~\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a0bab670e19bd5d3c7c43fc0add594c07}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+start}}(xa, \mbox{\hyperlink{kvm_2svm_2pmu_8c_a6053293001e6dbdc266facbd3024c21d}{index}}, entry, 0)}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a02212a367566f5b1ba22d6db87edcd25}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} -\/ Iterate over present entries in an X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index of @entry. @entry\+: Entry retrieved from array.

During the iteration, @entry will have the value of the entry stored in @xa at @index. You may modify @index during the iteration if you want to skip or reprocess indices. It is safe to modify the array during the iteration. At the end of the iteration, @entry will be set to N\+U\+LL and @index will have a value less than or equal to max.

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a02212a367566f5b1ba22d6db87edcd25}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} is O(n.\+log(n)) while \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad84fafa9a0297072b31cfcfbf6da5580}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} is O(n). You have to handle your own locking with \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad84fafa9a0297072b31cfcfbf6da5580}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}}, and if you have to unlock after each iteration, it will also end up being O(n.\+log(n)). \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a02212a367566f5b1ba22d6db87edcd25}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} will spin if it hits a retry entry; if you intend to see retry entries, you should use the \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad84fafa9a0297072b31cfcfbf6da5580}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} iterator instead. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad84fafa9a0297072b31cfcfbf6da5580}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} iterator will expand into more inline code than \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a02212a367566f5b1ba22d6db87edcd25}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}}.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the R\+CU lock. \mbox{\Hypertarget{xarray_8h_ac112b1208a63e7a2a838de0891d5ae8f}\label{xarray_8h_ac112b1208a63e7a2a838de0891d5ae8f}}

{\bfseries Value\+:}

\DoxyCodeLine{    \textcolor{keywordflow}{for} (\mbox{\hyperlink{kvm_2svm_2pmu_8c_a6053293001e6dbdc266facbd3024c21d}{index}} = 0, entry = \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a037a4d5fb4a6f2cb5cfb168b7adc61ec}{xa\_find}}(xa, \&\mbox{\hyperlink{kvm_2svm_2pmu_8c_a6053293001e6dbdc266facbd3024c21d}{index}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{vdso_2limits_8h_a41c51926a1997aab3503f9083935e06c}{ULONG\_MAX}}, filter); \(\backslash\)}

\end{DoxyCode}
\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac112b1208a63e7a2a838de0891d5ae8f}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} -\/ Iterate over marked entries in an X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index of @entry. @entry\+: Entry retrieved from array. @filter\+: Selection criterion.

During the iteration, @entry will have the value of the entry stored in @xa at @index. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} iteration will skip all entries in the array which do not match @filter. You may modify @index during the iteration if you want to skip or reprocess indices. It is safe to modify the array during the iteration. At the end of the iteration, @entry will be set to N\+U\+LL and @index will have a value less than or equal to max.

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac112b1208a63e7a2a838de0891d5ae8f}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} is O(n.\+log(n)) while \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a6fe251a2174b9b8967736339beb82800}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} is O(n). You have to handle your own locking with \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad84fafa9a0297072b31cfcfbf6da5580}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}}, and if you have to unlock after each iteration, it will also end up being O(n.\+log(n)). \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac112b1208a63e7a2a838de0891d5ae8f}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} will spin if it hits a retry entry; if you intend to see retry entries, you should use the \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a6fe251a2174b9b8967736339beb82800}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} iterator instead. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a6fe251a2174b9b8967736339beb82800}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} iterator will expand into more inline code than \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac112b1208a63e7a2a838de0891d5ae8f}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}}.

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad84fafa9a0297072b31cfcfbf6da5580}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} -\/ Iterate over a range of an X\+Array. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @entry\+: Entry retrieved from the array. @max\+: Maximum index to retrieve from array.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} loop body will be executed for each entry present in the xarray between the current xas position and @max. @entry will be set to the entry retrieved from the xarray. It is safe to delete entries from the array in the loop body. You should hold either the R\+CU lock or the xa\+\_\+lock while iterating. If you need to drop the lock, call \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a00880617477286d0196f06335fab3e97}{xas\+\_\+pause()}} first. \mbox{\Hypertarget{xarray_8h_aff0f90df898353e23346abe122867893}\label{xarray_8h_aff0f90df898353e23346abe122867893}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{xas\_for\_each\_conflict}{xas\_for\_each\_conflict}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \#define xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+conflict(\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{}]{xas,  }\item[{}]{entry }\end{DoxyParamCaption})~\mbox{\hyperlink{decode__rs_8c_a1761c7dde471cf88247e7c0255afca66}{while}} ((entry = \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_acd23b2d7c8b318bb35b35357082b2931}{xas\+\_\+find\+\_\+conflict}}(xas)))}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_aff0f90df898353e23346abe122867893}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+conflict()}} -\/ Iterate over a range of an X\+Array. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @entry\+: Entry retrieved from the array.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} loop body will be executed for each entry in the X\+Array that lies within the range specified by @xas. If the loop terminates normally, @entry will be N\+U\+LL. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} user may break out of the loop, which will leave @entry set to the conflicting entry. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller may also call xa\+\_\+set\+\_\+err() to exit the loop while setting an error to record the reason. \mbox{\Hypertarget{xarray_8h_a6fe251a2174b9b8967736339beb82800}\label{xarray_8h_a6fe251a2174b9b8967736339beb82800}}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a6fe251a2174b9b8967736339beb82800}{xas\+\_\+for\+\_\+each\+\_\+marked()}} -\/ Iterate over a range of an X\+Array. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @entry\+: Entry retrieved from the array. @max\+: Maximum index to retrieve from array. @mark\+: Mark to search for.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} loop body will be executed for each marked entry in the xarray between the current xas position and @max. @entry will be set to the entry retrieved from the xarray. It is safe to delete entries from the array in the loop body. You should hold either the R\+CU lock or the xa\+\_\+lock while iterating. If you need to drop the lock, call \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a00880617477286d0196f06335fab3e97}{xas\+\_\+pause()}} first. \mbox{\Hypertarget{xarray_8h_a9c61f08daaa0f4a72655a3fdb8ced8fe}\label{xarray_8h_a9c61f08daaa0f4a72655a3fdb8ced8fe}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{xa\_update\_node\_t}{xa\_update\_node\_t}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily typedef void($\ast$ xa\+\_\+update\+\_\+node\+\_\+t) (struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__node}{xa\+\_\+node}} $\ast$\mbox{\hyperlink{structnode}{node}})}

typedef xa\+\_\+update\+\_\+node\+\_\+t -\/ A callback function from the X\+Array. @node\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} node which is being processed

This function is called every time the X\+Array updates the count of present and value entries in a node. It allows advanced users to maintain the private\+\_\+list in the node.

Context\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} xa\+\_\+lock is held and interrupts may be disabled. Implementations should not drop the xa\+\_\+lock, nor re-\/enable interrupts.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_xa\_alloc()}{\_\_xa\_alloc()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} \mbox{\hyperlink{compiler__attributes_8h_af7045eee1ccd9074250c3eba96f769ac}{\+\_\+\+\_\+must\+\_\+check}} \+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+alloc (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxarray}{xarray}} $\ast$}]{xa,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_a10e94b422ef0c20dcdec20d31a1f5049}{u32}} $\ast$}]{id,  }\item[{void $\ast$}]{entry,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__limit}{xa\+\_\+limit}}}]{limit,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ac7af3d394e1fddbf83385f928a7e41ab}{gfp\+\_\+t}}}]{gfp }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a5ea87847c933d0a37d1a05726747af2c}{\+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+alloc()}} -\/ Find somewhere to store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @id\+: Pointer to ID. @limit\+: Range for allocated ID. @entry\+: New entry. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Finds an empty entry in @xa between @limit.min and @limit.max, stores the index into the @id pointer, then stores the entry at that index. A concurrent lookup will not see an uninitialised @id.

Context\+: Any context. Expects xa\+\_\+lock to be held on entry. May release and reacquire xa\+\_\+lock if @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 on success, -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated or -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if there are no free entries in @limit. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} \mbox{\hyperlink{compiler__attributes_8h_af7045eee1ccd9074250c3eba96f769ac}{\+\_\+\+\_\+must\+\_\+check}} \+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+cyclic (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxarray}{xarray}} $\ast$}]{xa,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_a10e94b422ef0c20dcdec20d31a1f5049}{u32}} $\ast$}]{id,  }\item[{void $\ast$}]{entry,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__limit}{xa\+\_\+limit}}}]{limit,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_a10e94b422ef0c20dcdec20d31a1f5049}{u32}} $\ast$}]{next,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ac7af3d394e1fddbf83385f928a7e41ab}{gfp\+\_\+t}}}]{gfp }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_ac2ce5ae886bb8755ed30a472d9350662}{\+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+cyclic()}} -\/ Find somewhere to store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @id\+: Pointer to ID. @entry\+: New entry. @limit\+: Range of allocated ID. @next\+: Pointer to next ID to allocate. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Finds an empty entry in @xa between @limit.min and @limit.max, stores the index into the @id pointer, then stores the entry at that index. A concurrent lookup will not see an uninitialised @id. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} search for an empty entry will start at @next and will wrap around if necessary.

Context\+: Any context. Expects xa\+\_\+lock to be held on entry. May release and reacquire xa\+\_\+lock if @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 if the allocation succeeded without wrapping. 1 if the allocation succeeded after wrapping, -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated or -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if there are no free entries in @limit. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a171ab4dd3f182e01217f50cb657fa86e}{\+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+cmpxchg()}} -\/ Store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array. @old\+: Old value to test against. @entry\+: New entry. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

You must already be holding the xa\+\_\+lock when calling this function. It will drop the lock if needed to allocate memory, and then reacquire it afterwards.

Context\+: Any context. Expects xa\+\_\+lock to be held on entry. May release and reacquire xa\+\_\+lock if @gfp flags permit. Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} old entry at this index or \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5d062b4e8ddb7dc78336dcbab5a9134c}{xa\+\_\+err()}} if an error happened. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a9ac8984ac32d48daa97da0278b6d0d29}{\+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+erase()}} -\/ Erase this entry from the X\+Array while locked. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array.

After this function returns, loading from @index will return N\+U\+LL. If the index is part of a multi-\/index entry, all indices will be erased and none of the entries will be part of a multi-\/index entry.

Context\+: Any context. Expects xa\+\_\+lock to be held on entry. Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry which used to be at this index. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a1cf427c7c40ee54110889dae4101700f}{\+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+insert()}} -\/ Store this entry in the X\+Array if no entry is present. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array. @entry\+: New entry. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Inserting a N\+U\+LL entry will store a reserved entry (like \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac48dbbe5f3f39dde8aad22e0bea2cf17}{xa\+\_\+reserve()}}) if no entry is present. Inserting will fail if a reserved entry is present, even though loading from this index will return N\+U\+LL.

Context\+: Any context. Expects xa\+\_\+lock to be held on entry. May release and reacquire xa\+\_\+lock if @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 if the store succeeded. -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if another entry was present. -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a16ec4e6c552946262b70ce78bbcc6c70}{\+\_\+\+\_\+xa\+\_\+store()}} -\/ Store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array. @entry\+: New entry. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

You must already be holding the xa\+\_\+lock when calling this function. It will drop the lock if needed to allocate memory, and then reacquire it afterwards.

Context\+: Any context. Expects xa\+\_\+lock to be held on entry. May release and reacquire xa\+\_\+lock if @gfp flags permit. Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} old entry at this index or \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5d062b4e8ddb7dc78336dcbab5a9134c}{xa\+\_\+err()}} if an error happened. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_aa307b009786cab04fcd57c4200eec47d}{xa\+\_\+alloc()}} -\/ Find somewhere to store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @id\+: Pointer to ID. @entry\+: New entry. @limit\+: Range of ID to allocate. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Finds an empty entry in @xa between @limit.min and @limit.max, stores the index into the @id pointer, then stores the entry at that index. A concurrent lookup will not see an uninitialised @id.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the xa\+\_\+lock. May sleep if the @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 on success, -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated or -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if there are no free entries in @limit. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a280e1512f2ba484b4e74f6b40887ee79}{xa\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+cyclic()}} -\/ Find somewhere to store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @id\+: Pointer to ID. @entry\+: New entry. @limit\+: Range of allocated ID. @next\+: Pointer to next ID to allocate. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Finds an empty entry in @xa between @limit.min and @limit.max, stores the index into the @id pointer, then stores the entry at that index. A concurrent lookup will not see an uninitialised @id. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} search for an empty entry will start at @next and will wrap around if necessary.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the xa\+\_\+lock. May sleep if the @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 if the allocation succeeded without wrapping. 1 if the allocation succeeded after wrapping, -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated or -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if there are no free entries in @limit. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a7d0ee3d21395883fd689f24c5883c278}{xa\+\_\+extract()}} -\/ Copy selected entries from the X\+Array into a normal array. @xa\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} source X\+Array to copy from. @dst\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} buffer to copy entries into. @start\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} first index in the X\+Array eligible to be selected. @max\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} last index in the X\+Array eligible to be selected. ~\newline
\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} maximum number of entries to copy. @filter\+: Selection criterion.

Copies up to ~\newline
 entries that match @filter from the X\+Array. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} copied entries will have indices between @start and @max, inclusive.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} @filter may be an X\+Array mark value, in which case entries which are marked with that mark will be copied. It may also be X\+A\+\_\+\+P\+R\+E\+S\+E\+NT, in which case all entries which are not N\+U\+LL will be copied.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entries returned may not represent a snapshot of the X\+Array at a moment in time. For example, if another thread stores to index 5, then index 10, calling \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a7d0ee3d21395883fd689f24c5883c278}{xa\+\_\+extract()}} may return the old contents of index 5 and the new contents of index 10. Indices not modified while this function is running will not be skipped.

If you need stronger guarantees, holding the xa\+\_\+lock across calls to this function will prevent concurrent modification.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the R\+CU lock. Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} number of entries copied. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 53
\mbox{\Hypertarget{xarray_8h_a037a4d5fb4a6f2cb5cfb168b7adc61ec}\label{xarray_8h_a037a4d5fb4a6f2cb5cfb168b7adc61ec}}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a549ad11737a3db78f0f6dc689342615c}{xa\+\_\+find()}} -\/ Search the X\+Array for an entry. @xa\+: X\+Array. @indexp\+: Pointer to an index. @max\+: Maximum index to search to. @filter\+: Selection criterion.

Finds the entry in @xa which matches the @filter, and has the lowest index that is at least @indexp and no more than @max. If an entry is found, @indexp is updated to be the index of the entry. This function is protected by the R\+CU read lock, so it may not find entries which are being simultaneously added. It will not return an X\+A\+\_\+\+R\+E\+T\+R\+Y\+\_\+\+E\+N\+T\+RY; if you need to see retry entries, use \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a48a227a3ec844a2d436e9dbda6d36c53}{xas\+\_\+find()}}.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the R\+CU lock. Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry, if found, otherwise N\+U\+LL. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a83911ff3eb3a632a004c702e3424cc58}{xa\+\_\+insert()}} -\/ Store this entry in the X\+Array unless another entry is already present. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array. @entry\+: New entry. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Inserting a N\+U\+LL entry will store a reserved entry (like \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac48dbbe5f3f39dde8aad22e0bea2cf17}{xa\+\_\+reserve()}}) if no entry is present. Inserting will fail if a reserved entry is present, even though loading from this index will return N\+U\+LL.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the xa\+\_\+lock. May sleep if the @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 if the store succeeded. -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if another entry was present. -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

Inserting a N\+U\+LL entry will store a reserved entry (like \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac48dbbe5f3f39dde8aad22e0bea2cf17}{xa\+\_\+reserve()}}) if no entry is present. Inserting will fail if a reserved entry is present, even though loading from this index will return N\+U\+LL.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the xa\+\_\+lock while disabling softirqs. May sleep if the @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 if the store succeeded. -\/E\+B\+U\+SY if another entry was present. -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if memory could not be allocated. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

Inserting a N\+U\+LL entry will store a reserved entry (like \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac48dbbe5f3f39dde8aad22e0bea2cf17}{xa\+\_\+reserve()}}) if no entry is present. Inserting will fail if a reserved entry is present, even though loading from this index will return N\+U\+LL.

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ac48dbbe5f3f39dde8aad22e0bea2cf17}{xa\+\_\+reserve()}} -\/ Reserve this index in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

Ensures there is somewhere to store an entry at @index in the array. If there is already something stored at @index, this function does nothing. If there was nothing there, the entry is marked as reserved. Loading from a reserved entry returns a N\+U\+LL pointer.

If you do not use the entry that you have reserved, call \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ae331444fe96d8842ba8b391262f6dbd9}{xa\+\_\+release()}} or \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a6139b90bc53e6a180bbd9b93bee7b274}{xa\+\_\+erase()}} to free any unnecessary memory.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the xa\+\_\+lock. May sleep if the @gfp flags permit. Return\+: 0 if the reservation succeeded or -\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM if it failed. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aa83e37dda96745ee79c20cbda3d9dc42}{xa\+\_\+store()}} -\/ Store this entry in the X\+Array. @xa\+: X\+Array. @index\+: Index into array. @entry\+: New entry. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

After this function returns, loads from this index will return @entry. Storing into an existing multi-\/index entry updates the entry of every index. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} marks associated with @index are unaffected unless @entry is N\+U\+LL.

Context\+: Any context. Takes and releases the xa\+\_\+lock. May sleep if the @gfp flags permit. Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} old entry at this index on success, xa\+\_\+err(-\/E\+I\+N\+V\+AL) if @entry cannot be stored in an X\+Array, or xa\+\_\+err(-\/E\+N\+O\+M\+EM) if memory allocation failed. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a54d4ddcb0146302e1d9257d6b7353d48}{xa\+\_\+tag\+\_\+pointer()}} -\/ Create an X\+Array entry for a tagged pointer. {\ttfamily }\+: Plain pointer. @tag\+: Tag value (0, 1 or 3).

If the user of the X\+Array prefers, they can tag their pointers instead of storing value entries. Three tags are available (0, 1 and 3). These are distinct from the xa\+\_\+mark\+\_\+t as they are not replicated up through the array and cannot be searched for.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily void$\ast$ xas\+\_\+find (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} $\ast$}]{xas,  }\item[{unsigned long}]{max }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a48a227a3ec844a2d436e9dbda6d36c53}{xas\+\_\+find()}} -\/ Find the next present entry in the X\+Array. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @max\+: Highest index to return.

If the @xas has not yet been walked to an entry, return the entry which has an index $>$= xas.\+xa\+\_\+index. If it has been walked, the entry currently being pointed at has been processed, and so we move to the next entry.

If no entry is found and the array is smaller than @max, the iterator is set to the smallest index not yet in the array. This allows @xas to be immediately passed to \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aa4318caf16c4066f2ef07c64d2313e11}{xas\+\_\+store()}}.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry, if found, otherwise N\+U\+LL. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily void$\ast$ xas\+\_\+find\+\_\+marked (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} $\ast$}]{xas,  }\item[{unsigned long}]{max,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a3394af60eca4a7c0e835a4cd74034171}{xa\+\_\+mark\+\_\+t}}}]{mark }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_ac6cd1b3174e811cebe1e742dad7c0bd1}{xas\+\_\+find\+\_\+marked()}} -\/ Find the next marked entry in the X\+Array. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @max\+: Highest index to return. @mark\+: Mark number to search for.

If the @xas has not yet been walked to an entry, return the marked entry which has an index $>$= xas.\+xa\+\_\+index. If it has been walked, the entry currently being pointed at has been processed, and so we return the first marked entry with an index $>$ xas.\+xa\+\_\+index.

If no marked entry is found and the array is smaller than @max, @xas is set to the bounds state and xas-\/$>$xa\+\_\+index is set to the smallest index not yet in the array. This allows @xas to be immediately passed to \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aa4318caf16c4066f2ef07c64d2313e11}{xas\+\_\+store()}}.

If no entry is found before @max is reached, @xas is set to the restart state.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry, if found, otherwise N\+U\+LL. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aea6a7922044a9708bafc7800b0e5bcda}{xas\+\_\+load()}} -\/ Load an entry from the X\+Array (advanced). @xas\+: X\+Array operation state.

Usually walks the @xas to the appropriate state to load the entry stored at xa\+\_\+index. However, it will do nothing and return N\+U\+LL if @xas is in an error state. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aea6a7922044a9708bafc7800b0e5bcda}{xas\+\_\+load()}} will never expand the tree.

If the \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} is set up to operate on a multi-\/index entry, \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aea6a7922044a9708bafc7800b0e5bcda}{xas\+\_\+load()}} may return N\+U\+LL or an internal entry, even if there are entries present within the range specified by @xas.

Context\+: Any context. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller should hold the xa\+\_\+lock or the R\+CU lock. Return\+: Usually an entry in the X\+Array, but see description for exceptions. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5b2c4d8c3c400d9e43c5133dc080113f}{xas\+\_\+next()}} -\/ Move state to next index. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state.

If the @xas was in an error state, it will remain in an error state and this function will return N\+U\+LL. If the @xas has never been walked, it will have the effect of calling \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5c49fae458c7245e67e68f6f4971a26e}{xas\+\_\+load()}}. Otherwise one will be added to the index and the state will be walked to the correct location in the array for the next operation.

If the iterator was referencing index U\+L\+O\+N\+G\+\_\+\+M\+AX, this function wraps around to 0.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry at the new index. This may be N\+U\+LL or an internal entry. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_afaa87723b8417d40fcf45b7330261ef9}{bool}} xas\+\_\+nomem (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} $\ast$}]{xas,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ac7af3d394e1fddbf83385f928a7e41ab}{gfp\+\_\+t}}}]{gfp }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a933ae36bb1cff0e4199d60c08b2b9aec}{xas\+\_\+nomem()}} -\/ Allocate memory if needed. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @gfp\+: Memory allocation flags.

If we need to add new nodes to the X\+Array, we try to allocate memory with G\+F\+P\+\_\+\+N\+O\+W\+A\+IT while holding the lock, which will usually succeed. If it fails, @xas is flagged as needing memory to continue. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller should drop the lock and call \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a933ae36bb1cff0e4199d60c08b2b9aec}{xas\+\_\+nomem()}}. If \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a933ae36bb1cff0e4199d60c08b2b9aec}{xas\+\_\+nomem()}} succeeds, the caller should retry the operation.

Forward progress is guaranteed as one node is allocated here and stored in the \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} where it will be found by \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_ae225f7c1aff13a8a23ee6a0de4a8dbbd}{xas\+\_\+alloc()}}. More nodes will likely be found in the slab allocator, but we do not tie them up here.

Return\+: true if memory was needed, and was successfully allocated. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily void xas\+\_\+pause (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} $\ast$}]{xas }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a26bd143f5afd5e52511ee14be680ef06}{xas\+\_\+pause()}} -\/ Pause a walk to drop a lock. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state.

Some users need to pause a walk and drop the lock they\textquotesingle{}re holding in order to yield to a higher priority thread or carry out an operation on an entry. Those users should call this function before they drop the lock. It resets the @xas to be suitable for the next iteration of the loop after the user has reacquired the lock. If most entries found during a walk require you to call \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a26bd143f5afd5e52511ee14be680ef06}{xas\+\_\+pause()}}, the \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a02212a367566f5b1ba22d6db87edcd25}{xa\+\_\+for\+\_\+each()}} iterator may be more appropriate.

Note that \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_a26bd143f5afd5e52511ee14be680ef06}{xas\+\_\+pause()}} only works for forward iteration. If a user needs to pause a reverse iteration, we will need a xas\+\_\+pause\+\_\+rev(). Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 149
Here is the caller graph for this function\+:
% FIG 150
\mbox{\Hypertarget{xarray_8h_a00b4643c854afa0b9d0467b96e9dcc9b}\label{xarray_8h_a00b4643c854afa0b9d0467b96e9dcc9b}}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a00b4643c854afa0b9d0467b96e9dcc9b}{xas\+\_\+prev()}} -\/ Move iterator to previous index. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state.

If the @xas was in an error state, it will remain in an error state and this function will return N\+U\+LL. If the @xas has never been walked, it will have the effect of calling \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5c49fae458c7245e67e68f6f4971a26e}{xas\+\_\+load()}}. Otherwise one will be subtracted from the index and the state will be walked to the correct location in the array for the next operation.

If the iterator was referencing index 0, this function wraps around to U\+L\+O\+N\+G\+\_\+\+M\+AX.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry at the new index. This may be N\+U\+LL or an internal entry. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ae0fddb6ac53227ba5525430caf97aa7e}{xas\+\_\+reload()}} -\/ Refetch an entry from the xarray. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state.

Use this function to check that a previously loaded entry still has the same value. This is useful for the lockless pagecache lookup where we walk the array with only the R\+CU lock to protect us, lock the page, then check that the page hasn\textquotesingle{}t moved since we looked it up.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller guarantees that @xas is still valid. If it may be in an error or restart state, call \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5c49fae458c7245e67e68f6f4971a26e}{xas\+\_\+load()}} instead.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} entry at this location in the xarray. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily static void xas\+\_\+reset (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}} $\ast$}]{xas }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a053761da3dddd3ac015d345412fa51eb}{xas\+\_\+reset()}} -\/ Reset an X\+Array operation state. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state.

Resets the error or walk state of the @xas so future walks of the array will start from the root. Use this if you have dropped the xarray lock and want to reuse the \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}}.

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a80e70289f94bbaa6b229b6ccce877edc}{xas\+\_\+retry()}} -\/ Retry the operation if appropriate. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @entry\+: Entry from xarray.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} advanced functions may sometimes return an internal entry, such as a retry entry or a zero entry. This function sets up the @xas to restart the walk from the head of the array if needed.

Context\+: Any context. Return\+: true if the operation needs to be retried. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_af2949ad11993a2257e2010597f187cf8}{xas\+\_\+set()}} -\/ Set up X\+Array operation state for a different index. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @index\+: New index into the X\+Array.

Move the operation state to refer to a different index. This will have the effect of starting a walk from the top; see \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a5b2c4d8c3c400d9e43c5133dc080113f}{xas\+\_\+next()}} to move to an adjacent index. Here is the caller graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_ad118cf39010b4b38a8929e90110abb18}{xas\+\_\+set\+\_\+err()}} -\/ Note an error in the \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}}. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @err\+: Negative error number.

Only call this function with a negative @err; zero or positive errors will probably not behave the way you think they should. If you want to clear the error from an \mbox{\hyperlink{structxa__state}{xa\+\_\+state}}, use \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8h_a053761da3dddd3ac015d345412fa51eb}{xas\+\_\+reset()}}. \mbox{\hyperlink{xarray_8c_aa4318caf16c4066f2ef07c64d2313e11}{xas\+\_\+store()}} -\/ Store this entry in the X\+Array. @xas\+: X\+Array operation state. @entry\+: New entry.

If @xas is operating on a multi-\/index entry, the entry returned by this function is essentially meaningless (it may be an internal entry or it may be N\+U\+LL, even if there are non-\/\+N\+U\+LL entries at some of the indices covered by the range). This is not a problem for any current users, and can be changed if needed.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} old entry at this index. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
